# envoy-go State — HISTORY ARCHIVE

> **This file is NOT a router. It carries NO live pointer. Nothing here is "what next."**
>
> **`docs/envoy-go/STATE.md` is the single source of truth for "what next"** (`BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §4.1 invariant 1). This archive exists only so the project's "disk is the only memory" doctrine (`BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md`, Step B *Never*: *"if it's not on disk, it does not exist for you"*) is honored byte-for-byte while STATE.md is restored to the **pointer** its own charter defines it as (`BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §4: *"STATE.md — pointer: active phase, last commit, next action"*).
>
> **Created at the ADR-0288 archival move (2026-07-16), off master `a1fe295a`.** See `DECISIONS.md` ADR-0288 for the rationale and the three-contradictory-pointers defect that forced it.
>
> ## ⚠️ EVERY POINTER, COUNT, AND `next-skill:` BELOW IS HISTORICAL AND STALE BY CONSTRUCTION.
>
> Do **NOT** grep this file for `next-skill:`, `lifecycle-state:`, `next-free ADR:`, `fuzzers:`, `active differential fixtures:`, or any count. Those greps are exactly what ADR-0288 exists to stop returning wrong answers. **Grep `STATE.md`.** Read this file only to reconstruct *how the project got somewhere* — never *where it is*.
>
> The content below is the **verbatim, unedited** `STATE.md` as of master `a1fe295a` (750,794 bytes, 384 lines), preserved in full. It includes the two fossilised "current state" blocks (the phase-42.1 block and the phase-46.1b counts block) that had become buried under ~330 lines of prepended backlog and were still advertising `next-skill: the 42.2 hedging BRAINSTORM` / `the 46.2 SPEC` at a time when the live phase was 66. Those blocks are the defect, retained here as the evidence.

D-ENV-PRECEDENCE custom-overrides-built-in + custom-vs-custom first-in-config-order-wins (arms B/C/D, IDENTICAL to phase 62); **D-ENV-DEDUP** — an `environment` tag that resolves to NOTHING but is FIRST in config order STILL RESERVES its config-order key slot, suppressing a later same-key tag (arm F — the reference dedups by key at config-LOAD, BEFORE value resolution); D-ENV-REJECT empty-`environment.name` PGV boot-reject (arm E, `.pb.validate.go:468` `min_len:1`; the reference ACCEPTS `environment` ⇒ the departure NARROWS to `metadata` alone). **The CENTRAL decision — D-ENV-RESOLVE-TIME — FLIPS the BRAINSTORM's anticipated Option A → Option B** (ADR-0044 empirical refinement, §1.1): the D-ENV-DEDUP + D-ENV-EMPTYVAL findings mean resolution can yield NOTHING for a tag that must still occupy its dedup slot, so the SPEC pins a `kindEnvironment` `CustomTagSpec` stored at parse (dedup-slot reserved NATURALLY via the existing append+`seen` path) + a `kindEnvironment` case in `ResolveCustomTags` (`os.LookupEnv`, omit-iff-empty, ignores `headerLookup`) — mirroring the reference's two-phase (dedup-then-resolve) model + the landed `kindRequestHeader` structure; value-equality + the **D-ENV-HARNESS** env-injection (testcontainers `ContainerRequest.Env` `harness.go:124` + `cmd.Env` `harness.go:252`, threaded per-fixture) WEIGHED + DEFERRED (not one-line-per-starter). the seven resolved D-questions incl. two the SPEC didn't settle (`record_headers_received_time` REJECTED — no accessor exposes header-arrival instant; `HostMount.Dir` — the harness needed a directory bind-mount, not a file). **THREE IMPL findings, each catching a plan-time gap:** **(IMPL-1)** two of Task 7's three deliberate reject-roster breaks were VACUOUS as designed (deleting the `streaming_grpc` case fell through to the defensive `default` arm; deleting the neither-match guard fell through to `matchpredicate.Compile(nil)`; both still rejected, so the `err==nil`-only `TestNew_RejectRoster` stayed green), hardened to assert per-arm error SUBSTRINGS (ADR-0080 arm-distinctness) — all 3 breaks now fire on their exact subtest. **(IMPL-2)** the ONE-SHARED-VALUE dynamic proof (Task 9) was VACUOUS against `validTap()`'s REQUEST-ONLY predicate (a decoder-only split still resolved True on the request arm alone → still 1 file/`rq_tapped` 1); fixed with a new `validTapReqAndResp()` `and_match{req x-tap=yes, resp :status=204}` so a decoder-only split stays Undetermined → 0 files. **(IMPL-3)** Go's `protojson` uses `internal/detrand`, which injects build-seeded whitespace randomization, so a literal byte-exact golden passes under plain `go test` but deterministically FAILS under `-race` (a different binary → a different detrand seed) — the PLAN mandated BOTH a byte-exact golden AND running the package under `-race`, physically incompatible; resolved (user-approved): the golden is compared as CANONICAL JSON (`encoding/json.Compact`) plus detrand-robust substring pins, the `want` golden string kept verbatim as documentation — this still catches an `EmitUnpopulated` regression (`"body":null` is a real token, verified live) and is build-independent; a correction to the SPEC §11.3/AMEND-TAP-JSON byte-exact pin (build-scoped, not cross-build), not a defect — the differential decodes payloads and never byte-compares, so it was never affected. content-type on a **204** SURVIVED both proxies end-to-end (D-TAP-SUBSET resolved as originally stated, NOT moved to the UNasserted list). **Exit counts:** stat surface **1200 → 1201** (+1, `http.<stat_prefix>.tap.rq_tapped`, enforced by the T7 +1 delta guard, not an absolute assertion) · fixtures **100 → 101** (`0099-http-tap-headers`) · fuzzers **52 → 53** (`FuzzMatchPredicateCompile`) · BackendKind tail **38** (+0) · DECISIONS tail **ADR-0273** (next-free **ADR-0274**) · new Go packages **2** (`internal/headermatch`, `internal/matchpredicate`) + the filter package `internal/filter/http/tap` · new go.mod modules **0** (`go mod tidy -diff` empty). All 14 deliberate breaks were re-performed BY THE CONTROLLER with `-count=1`; the `0099` breaks all fired on the **subject** side — see `PROGRESS-56.1.md`'s ledger for the literal failing text. **Doc:** `docs/envoy-go/phases/56-http-tap-filter/{SPEC-56.1.md,PLAN-56.1.md,PROGRESS-56.1.md}`. **Next → the phase-56.2 (bodies) leg.**

- **prior active-phase:** `phase 56.1 (http-tap-filter, headers leg) PLAN done` (2026-07-10) — the NINTH Observability-family row's FIRST leg PLANNED (lifecycle-state 2 → 3; docs-only, worktree `.worktrees/phase-56.1-plan`, branch `phase-56.1-http-tap-filter-plan`; `PLAN-56.1.md` + `PROGRESS-56.1.md`, the phase-44 per-leg convention). **The ADR-0045 escape valve is DISCHARGED: 15 tasks — AT the `~15` ceiling, margin ZERO.** An honest first decomposition came to 16; two SEMANTIC folds (`record_downstream_connection` into the trace-assembly task; the reject-roster and byte-stability unit tests into the tasks that introduce them) bring it to 15. A further 56.1a/56.1b **by-layer** sub-split was CONSIDERED and REJECTED — the BRAINSTORM's *(Q6)* already ruled it out because it would strand `internal/matchpredicate` as dead library code with no differential surface, and the phase-46.1a/46.1b precedent does not transfer (there BOTH halves had an observable emit surface; here the library half has none). **56.2 MUST NOT absorb spillover from this leg**; if an IMPL task grows a second deliverable, split it and RE-OPEN the gate. **All SIX §12 D-questions resolved, plus a SEVENTH the SPEC's "a per-side temp dir" hand-wave concealed:** **D-TAP-DIRMOUNT** — `file_per_tap` writes one file per matching stream with an unpredictable `<trace_id>` name, so the reference (in Docker) needs a **DIRECTORY** bind-mount; but `fixture.HostMount` is `{HostPath, ContainerPath}` and the runner unconditionally pre-creates `HostPath` as a FILE (`os.OpenFile(O_CREATE|O_WRONLY)`; `O_WRONLY` on a directory returns `EISDIR`). The PLAN adds `HostMount.Dir bool` + a runner `MkdirAll(0o777)`/`Chmod` branch at BOTH pre-create sites (`runner_test.go:1146` and `:2110`), with a non-sticky container dir `/envoy-go-test/taps` (the `0006` EACCES lesson: envoy runs as uid 101 and `/tmp` in the image is sticky + world-writable under `fs.protected_regular=2`). **This is TEST-harness surgery, NOT production framework surgery** — the framework-zero-touch pin scopes to the HTTP-filter framework and HCM; the `0006` file-mount is the regression gate. **D-TAP-DEPTHCAP = 32** (root = depth 1; a depth-33 AND a depth-512 fuzzer seed must exercise it — a cap that is never exercised is not a cap). **D-TAP-TRACEID** = a process-local monotonic `atomic.Uint64` + an `O_EXCL` open (the id is never asserted cross-side, so `crypto/rand` would add an error path for zero benefit). **D-TAP-RECORDFLAGS** — HONOR `record_downstream_connection` (both address fields plumbable via `DecoderFilterCallbacks.DownstreamLocalAddr()` `callbacks.go:113` / `DownstreamRemoteAddr()` `:103`); **REJECT `record_headers_received_time: true`** — grep-proven that NO accessor anywhere exposes the per-direction header-arrival instant (neither callbacks interface returns a `time.Time`, `FilterChain` has no time field, `chain.go` does not import `"time"`), and in an ADR-0080 tree "defer" *means* an explicit reject. **D-TAP-SUBSET** — request ⊇ `{:method GET, :path /tap, x-tap yes, x-backend-status 204}`, response ⊇ `{:status 204, content-type text/plain}`; `:authority` (ports differ), `:scheme` (never plumbed), `x-request-id`/`x-forwarded-proto`/`x-envoy-*`/`date`/`server`/`connection`/`user-agent` and header ORDER are UNasserted boundaries. **D-TAP-EMITSITE** — ONE shared `*tapFilter` in both `HTTPFilter` fields and **NO** defensive `sync.Once` (it would be dead code no test could drive to a second call); proven instead by three tests, incl. a `chain_test.go` regression pinning that an Encoder-only `OnDestroy` is UNREACHABLE when a Decoder is present. **D-TAP-PATHPREFIX** — `os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(path_prefix), 0o755)` at filter-parse time, reject on error (fail at boot, not silently drop every trace at stream end; a documented DEPARTURE — the reference's behavior here was not probed). **The `0099` fixture needs NO new BackendKind (tail stays 38):** the landed `HTTPStatusHeader` (kind 3) backend, driven with `x-backend-status: 204`, was VERIFIED by raw-socket probe to emit a bodyless `204 No Content` + `Content-Type: text/plain` with **no `Content-Length` and zero body bytes** — exactly the AMEND-TAP-BODY shape, and `BackendCount()` returns 1 with the backend genuinely used. All trace assertions live in **`AssertStats`**, NEVER `AssertSubject` (which fires only on the reference-less path — `reference_differential_asserter_dispatch`). **TWO DEFECTS WERE CAUGHT BY ADVERSARIAL REVIEW BEFORE ANY CODE WAS WRITTEN**, each of which would have shipped a GREEN-BUT-VACUOUS proof of a load-bearing property: **(i) the `body`-ABSENT assertion had NO liveness proof.** The planned deliberate break (`EmitUnpopulated: true`) renders `"body": null`, and `protojson.Unmarshal` maps a JSON `null` message field back to a **nil** `Body` — identical to an omitted field. `EmitUnpopulated` is a superset of `EmitDefaultValues`, so `raw_value: ""` and `trailers: []` still render. Under the break the entire `0099` fixture stayed **GREEN — no assertion fired at all** — and since the fixture is deliberately bodyless on both sides, assertion (5) is a tautology on every normal run. Fixed by splitting the property: a decoded `GetBody()==nil` check **plus a RAW-BYTES check that the file contains no `"body"` key**, with break (c) proving the raw-bytes check and a NEW break (c′) (fabricate a real `Body{AsString:"x"}`) proving the decoded one. A decode-based check structurally cannot distinguish "omitted" from "null". **(ii) the ONE-SHARED-VALUE proof was self-defeating.** The emit-once test downcast `hf.Decoder.(*tapFilter)` and fed BOTH header sets into that value — so under the two-value split it was meant to detect, `hf.Decoder` *is* the fresh value, it received both header sets, matched, emitted, and the break **PASSED**. Fixed by driving the INTERFACE values (`hf.Decoder.DecodeHeaders`, `hf.Encoder.EncodeHeaders`) so a split lands the two header sets on two different values and `Destroy()`'s Decoder-only branch emits nothing. (A third proposed break — "drop `Decoder` entirely; must PASS" — would have *panicked* on a nil interface conversion, not passed; removed.) Both reviewers were instructed to re-derive every claim from source and NEVER to check a citation against the document that made it; both independently found defect (i), one proved it by execution, and the controller re-verified it independently — **`feedback_brief_citations_not_evidence` paying out a SECOND time on this row** (the SPEC's own first draft was refuted the same way). The PLAN additionally refutes `builtins.go`'s own doc comment: **`cmd/envoy-go/main.go` performs NO HTTP-filter registration** (grep-proven — `builtins.go` is the sole registration site, `boot.go:65` the sole freeze; `main.go:322`'s `Freeze()` is the STATS registry), and confirms `FactoryCtx` has **no `BaseDir`** field. Counts UNCHANGED at this PLAN (docs-only): stat surface **1200** · fixtures **100** · fuzzers **52** · BackendKind tail **38** · DECISIONS tail **ADR-0272** (next-free **ADR-0273**; the §Decision/§Consequences bodies land at the 56.1 IMPL per ADR-0044). Row 56 STAYS `in-progress` (flips `done` only at the 56.2 IMPL — `reference_roadmap_split_phase_row_done` + ADR-0106, no parent rollup). **Next → the phase-56.1 IMPL.**

- **prior active-phase:** `phase 56.1 (http-tap-filter, headers leg) SPEC done` (2026-07-10) — the NINTH Observability-family row's FIRST leg SPEC'd (lifecycle-state 1 → 2; docs-only, worktree `.worktrees/phase-56.1-spec`, branch `phase-56.1-http-tap-filter-spec`; the phase-44 per-leg convention — one shared `BRAINSTORM.md`, then `SPEC-56.1.md`/`PLAN-56.1.md`/`PROGRESS-56.1.md`, plus a new `README.md`). **All TEN §10 D-TAP-* pins were EXECUTED IN-SESSION against `envoyproxy/envoy:contrib-v1.37.2`** (ADR-0004/ADR-0227) on a Docker **bridge** network, `--concurrency 1`, a **FRESH probe container per arm** (`feedback_probe_fresh_container_per_arm`), a `mccutchen/go-httpbin` backend and a `curlimages/curl` sidecar driver (the envoy image ships NO curl/wget/nc). **They CONFIRM the buffered end-of-stream-artifact model and CORRECT the charter on TWELVE counts (`AMEND-TAP-*`).** **The load-bearing amendments:** **(1) AMEND-TAP-BODY** — the reference ALWAYS captures bodies and there is NO suppression knob (`max_buffered_{rx,tx}_bytes: 0` still emits `{"as_string":"","truncated":true}`), BUT a **ZERO-LENGTH body message OMITS the `body` field entirely** (measured two ways: a bodyless GET → `request keys: ['headers','trailers']`; a **204** response → `response keys: ['headers','trailers']`) ⇒ a **bodyless GET → 204** yields a structurally HEADERS-ONLY trace on BOTH sides, which is what makes `0099` a genuine field-by-field cross-side comparison and turns `request.body`/`response.body` ABSENT into a POSITIVE breakable assertion. **This decides the fixture.** **(2) AMEND-TAP-NOEARLYEMIT** — the BRAINSTORM §2.6 explicitly flagged short-circuit/early-emit as UNSETTLED; the `orshort` probe (`or_match{[http_request_headers_match(x-tap==yes), http_response_headers_match(:status==999)]}` — request arm TRUE at decode, response arm never true) still emitted a trace carrying BOTH `request` AND `response` ⇒ the reference **NEVER early-emits**; the trace is ALWAYS the WHOLE stream, assembled at stream end. The tri-state is needed to RESOLVE the tree, NOT to decide WHEN to emit. **(3) AMEND-TAP-JSON** — the `file_per_tap` JSON is BYTE-EXACT `protojson.MarshalOptions{Multiline:true, Indent:" ", UseProtoNames:true, **EmitDefaultValues:true**}` + a trailing `\n` (proven by round-tripping a real reference trace: `EXACT BYTE MATCH (ignoring trailing \n): true`; 6 separate processes → 1 sha256). **`EmitUnpopulated:true` is WRONG** (it emits `"body": null` / `"headers_received_time": null` / `"downstream_connection": null`, which the reference never does). **(4) AMEND-TAP-PSEUDO (contains the leg's single most dangerous IMPL trap)** — `:scheme` is NEVER injected into the filter-visible request header map (only `:method`/`:authority`/`:path` are; the `h2/stream.go` `:scheme` hits are the WIRE DECODER) ⇒ UNassert it cross-side; `:status` is NOT in the encode header map (`connection.go:736` says so verbatim) and must come from `EncoderFilterCallbacks.ResponseStatus()` (ADR-0196); and **the WIRE-LEAK TRAP** — `connection.go:738` hands `merged` (built :733) to `RunEncodeHeaders` and then `ReconcileOrderedHeaders(resp.Headers, merged)` at `:741`, so adding a synthetic `:status` to `merged` would emit a literal `:status` header ON THE WIRE; the tap filter MUST build a COPY. Go's `http.Header` also canonicalizes keys and has no stable iteration order ⇒ tap lowercases every key and sorts. **(5) AMEND-TAP-PRECEDENCE** — `match` (f4) WINS over the DEPRECATED `match_config` (f1), which is silently IGNORED when both are set (arm `prec`: `match_config: any_match:true` + `match: x-tap=="NOPE"` on a request carrying `x-tap: yes` → `TAP FILE COUNT: 0`); `match_config` ALONE taps; **NEITHER set → the reference BOOT-REJECTS** (`Neither match nor match_config is set in TapConfig`) — a PARITY reject arm the charter never anticipated. **(6) AMEND-TAP-TAPENABLED** — `TapConfig.tap_enabled` (f3, `config/tap/v3/common.pb.go:143`) is a field the charter **never mentions**; probed INERT across three arms (0%/HUNDRED with and without `runtime_key`, and 100%/HUNDRED) — all tapped. envoy-go REJECTS it explicitly. **(7) AMEND-TAP-REJECTS** — the reject roster splits **PARITY** (the reference ALSO boot-rejects: neither-match-nor-match_config; `sinks` count != 1 [PGV `value must contain exactly 1 item(s)`]; `streaming_admin` w/o admin; `buffered_admin` w/o admin; an unregistered `custom_sink`) vs **DEPARTURE** (the reference ACCEPTS or CRASHES, envoy-go rejects anyway per ADR-0080: `admin_config` [boots inert]; `streaming: true` [boots, writes 3 concatenated JSON docs]; the 2 trailer arms [boots and honors]; the 2 generic_body arms [boots AND matches]; the 3 PROTO_* formats [boots and honors, `.pb`/`.pb_length_delimited`/`.pb_text`]; `tap_enabled`; `match_config`). **The sharpest single finding: a `streaming_grpc` sink makes the REFERENCE ABORT ITS OWN PROCESS** — exit 139, `[critical][assert] source/extensions/common/tap/tap_config_base.cc:119] panic: not implemented`, reproduced TWICE with a well-formed HTTP/2 sink cluster (once `PROTO_BINARY`+buffered, once `JSON_BODY_AS_STRING`+`streaming:true`) ⇒ envoy-go's reject is strictly SAFER, and the deferred `streaming_grpc` row is blocked on a reference that does not crash. **(8-12) the counting/citation corrections:** `OutputConfig.sinks` is PGV-constrained to EXACTLY 1 (AMEND-TAP-SINKS); `config/route/v3.HeaderMatcher` has **8** `HeaderMatchSpecifier` oneof arms (5 DEPRECATED) + `invert_match` + `treat_missing_header_as_empty`, and `internal/rbac/evaluator.go:855` handles **8** not the charter's 11 (that was a whole-file grep including a comment), while `csrf`/`hcm/config.go` reference HeaderMatcher only in COMMENTS — real code use outside rbac/ratelimit/oauth2 is `fault` alone (AMEND-TAP-ARMCOUNT); `builtins.go`'s bulk block is `:44-63` and registers **20** filters today (one, `envoygotest`, is test-support) so tap is the **21st registered / 20th production**, not the charter's "19 → 20" (AMEND-TAP-FILTERCOUNT); the HTTP registry is frozen at **`internal/boot/boot.go:65`**, NOT `main.go` (whose `Freeze` at :322 is the STATS registry — `registry.go:58`'s own doc comment is STALE) (AMEND-TAP-FREEZE); and a bare `common.pb.go` names **THREE** different files (`extensions/common/tap/v3` / `config/tap/v3` / `data/tap/v3`), there are **TWO distinct `MatchPredicate` protos** (the DEPRECATED package-local `config/tap/v3` one vs the `config/common/matcher/v3` one this row compiles), and `data/tap/v3.TraceWrapper` is a **4-arm** oneof (AMEND-TAP-PROTOPATHS). **The pinned architecture (ADR-0273 §Context drafted at SPEC §13 per ADR-0044):** `internal/headermatch` (a fresh EXPORTED 8-arm HeaderMatcher carrying its OWN `StringMatcher` evaluation — no shared one exists; MIGRATE NOBODY per Q5) + `internal/matchpredicate` (the recursive tri-state compiler+incremental evaluator: `and`=False-if-any-False/True-if-all-True/else-Undetermined, `or`=dual, `not` passes Undetermined through, still-Undetermined resolves False at stream end — a total-function guard, near-unreachable since a response always exists) with a MANDATORY recursion DEPTH CAP (value a PLAN question; RECOMMEND 32) + `internal/filter/http/tap` housing an **in-package** `filePerTapSink` (**D-TAP-SINKPLACEMENT resolved: in-package**, not a sibling `internal/tapsink` — one consumer). **The ONE-SHARED-VALUE constraint on the emit site (LOAD-BEARING — and the INVERSE of the obvious guess; caught by adversarial review of the controller's OWN brief, a `feedback_brief_citations_not_evidence` catch):** there is NO `OnStreamEnd`; the teardown hook is `OnDestroy()`, declared on BOTH `StreamDecoderFilter` (`types.go:59`) and `StreamEncoderFilter` (`:70`). The SPEC's first draft asserted "`FilterChain.Destroy()` fires both, so a both-sided filter's `OnDestroy` fires TWICE — once-guard it." **That is FALSE.** `Destroy()` (`chain.go:665`) is `destroyOnce`-guarded (`:292`/`:666`) and its loop is `if f.Decoder != nil { f.Decoder.OnDestroy() } else if f.Encoder != nil { f.Encoder.OnDestroy() }` (`chain.go:668-672`) — **mutually exclusive branches**, so a both-sided filter's `OnDestroy` fires **EXACTLY ONCE** (Decoder branch) and no guard is needed for correctness. **The REAL hazard is the inverse: an Encoder-only `OnDestroy` is UNREACHABLE whenever a Decoder is present** — so a natural-looking two-value decomposition (`Decoder: &decodeSide{}, Encoder: &encodeSide{}`) would silently NEVER emit. ⇒ tap MUST install ONE shared `*tapFilter` value in BOTH `HTTPFilter` fields (the landed `compressor` precedent, `compressor/doc.go:43` + `compressor.go:304`) and hang the emit off its single `OnDestroy`. **Stat surface pinned +1:** exactly ONE counter `http.<stat_prefix>.tap.rq_tapped` (the `.tap.` segment HARDCODED — renaming the `http_filters[]` entry does not change it; registered at filter-parse so it reads 0 with no taps), and **ZERO new SN rule** — running `stats.ExtractTags` in-tree on `http.hcm_probe.tap.rq_tapped` already yields `name="http.tap_rq_tapped" tags=[{envoy_http_conn_manager_prefix hcm_probe}]`, exactly matching the reference's `envoy_http_tap_rq_tapped{envoy_http_conn_manager_prefix="hcm_probe"}`. `reference_dynamic_stat_name_charset_guard` is explicitly NOT applicable (no stat name derives from wire bytes). **Differential (`0099-http-tap-headers`, fixtures 100 → 101):** a GET→204 headers-only design; glob `<path_prefix>*` and compare the decoded `TraceWrapper` PAYLOAD, never filenames (they embed a proxy-internal `<trace_id>`; scheme pinned `<path_prefix>_<trace_id>.<ext>`, ext format-dependent) per `reference_streaming_sink_differential_framing`; assert trace COUNT == N, `rq_tapped` == N, a named lowercased header subset, `body` ABSENT, `trailers == []`, `downstream_connection` ABSENT, and NO trace for non-matching streams; `BackendCount() >= 1` (`reference_differential_backendcount_min_one`), BackendKind stays **38**. **The §6 strict-reject roster is proven by UNIT TESTS, not fixture arms** (`reference_differential_fixture_dispatch_constraint` — one fixture dir = ONE runner branch; the phase-55 precedent). FOUR deliberate breaks, each `-count=1` and `t.Errorf`-per-property: (a) emit-at-`DecodeHeaders`, (b) predicate-always-true (two independent properties), (c) `EmitUnpopulated` (⇒ `"body": null`), (d) populate `Message.trailers`. **Fuzzer +1:** `FuzzMatchPredicateCompile` (52 → 53), which must exercise the depth cap. **The trailers COVERAGE BOUNDARY holds and is INVISIBLE in the differential** — `EmitDefaultValues` renders an empty repeated `trailers` as `[]` byte-identically on both sides, so the `trailers == []` assertion is cross-side-EXACT even though envoy-go can never observe a trailer (`RunDecodeTrailers` `chain.go:454` / `RunEncodeTrailers` `:621`, ZERO non-test callers — the never-done HCM "Task 18"). Framework-zero-touch preserved; scope NOT widened. **FINAL ADR-0045 split-gate re-check: the chartered 56.1/56.2 by-concern split STANDS** — the SPEC's decomposition lands at **~14 tasks**, AT the gate, not past it. Escape valve re-armed explicitly: *if the PLAN's decomposition exceeds ~15 tasks, re-open ADR-0045 before writing code.* **Counts UNCHANGED at this SPEC (docs-only; re-verified against master tip `08b736a3`):** stat surface **1200** / fixtures **100** / fuzzers **52** / BackendKind **38** / DECISIONS tail **ADR-0272** (next-free **ADR-0273**). **Anticipated at the 56.1 IMPL:** stat surface **1201** (+1) / fixtures **101** (`0099-http-tap-headers`) / fuzzers **53** (`FuzzMatchPredicateCompile`) / BackendKind **38** (+0) / DECISIONS **ADR-0273** (next-free ADR-0274) / **2 new packages** + the filter package / **ZERO new go.mod modules** (`go mod tidy -diff` anticipated EMPTY). **Row 56 STAYS `in-progress`** (flips `done` only at the 56.2 FINAL-leg IMPL six-gate — `reference_roadmap_split_phase_row_done` + ADR-0106, no parent rollup); the Observability FAMILY STAYS OPEN. **Docs:** `docs/envoy-go/phases/56-http-tap-filter/SPEC-56.1.md` + `README.md`. **NEXT: the phase-56.1 PLAN** (decompose §10 into a TDD spine; resolve the SIX §12 D-questions — D-TAP-DEPTHCAP / D-TAP-TRACEID / D-TAP-RECORDFLAGS / D-TAP-SUBSET / D-TAP-EMITSITE / D-TAP-PATHPREFIX; FINAL ADR-0045 re-check against the concrete decomposition; `superpowers:writing-plans`).

- **prior active-phase:** `phase 56 (http-tap-filter) BRAINSTORM done` (2026-07-10) — the NINTH Observability-family row CHARTERED (lifecycle-state 0 → 1; docs-only, worktree `.worktrees/phase-56-brainstorm`, branch `phase-56-http-tap-filter-brainstorm`; the loop was RE-OPENED by a human pick after phase 55 landed). **The pick (Q0):** the HTTP tap filter (`envoy.filters.http.tap`) over three DECLINED alternatives — a narrow-CDS xDS-family opener, the `graphite` sink, and an Operational-tooling admin-API reload/validate endpoint. **The 7 settled pins (Q0–Q6):** (Q0) candidate = the tap filter; (Q1 — LOAD-BEARING) SCOPE TRAILERS OUT and stay framework-zero-touch (boot-reject `http_request_trailers_match`/`http_response_trailers_match`, never populate `Message.trailers`, record the omission as an explicit BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT coverage boundary, defer HCM "Task 18" to its own framework-surgery row); (Q2) `file_per_tap` as the ONLY output sink (explicit reject for the other four `OutputSink` arms); (Q3) a BUFFERED trace in JSON format (honor `streaming: false` only; boot-reject `streaming: true` / `HttpStreamedTraceSegment`); (Q4) 6 of the 10 `MatchPredicate` arms — `and_match`/`or_match`/`not_match`/`any_match`/`http_request_headers_match`/`http_response_headers_match`, rejecting the 2 trailer + 2 generic-body arms; (Q5) a NEW shared EXPORTED `internal/headermatch` that MIGRATES NOBODY (rbac/ratelimit/oauth2 left untouched, migration documented as future debt); (Q6) a BY-CONCERN split — 56.1 headers / 56.2 bodies — that CONSUMES the ADR-0045 gate (rejecting a by-LAYER split, which would strand dead library code with no differential surface, and a single flat ~25-task row). **TWO LOAD-BEARING findings, called out as such:** (1) **Trailers are structurally invisible to envoy-go's HTTP filters.** `StreamDecoderFilter.DecodeTrailers` / `FilterChain.RunDecodeTrailers` (chain.go:454) and `RunEncodeTrailers` (chain.go:621) exist with ZERO non-test callers; HCM never dispatches them (`internal/filter/hcm/connection.go:565`, `internal/filter/hcm/h2dispatch.go:501-503` — the never-done "Task 18", written at phase 04). Tap is the FIRST feature whose PURPOSE collides with it, so phase 56 stays framework-zero-touch: the 2 trailer `MatchPredicate` arms are boot-rejected (ADR-0080) and `Message.trailers` is never populated — a documented COVERAGE BOUNDARY, with HCM trailer plumbing deferred to its own future framework-surgery row (the `reference_close_direction_framework_gap` precedent). (2) **A buffered trace is an END-OF-STREAM ARTIFACT.** `http_response_headers_match` cannot resolve at decode time, so the predicate tree is evaluated INCREMENTALLY and the assembled trace is emitted at stream end once the match resolves — never at `DecodeHeaders`. Corollary: a `not_match`/`and_match` over a response arm needs a TRI-STATE (match / no-match / undetermined) per node, not a bool; the exact mechanism (short-circuit / early-emit) is forwarded to the SPEC/PLAN. **The correction the survey produced:** there is **no network tap FILTER in Envoy at all** — L4 tap is a transport socket (`envoy/extensions/transport_sockets/tap` PRESENT; `envoy/extensions/filters/network/tap/v3` ABSENT), so a "network tap filter" was a category error, scoped out BY CONSTRUCTION rather than by choice. **Fixture-design constraint:** `FilePerTapSink` has ONE field (`path_prefix`) and "outputs a discrete file for every tapped stream", so per-stream filenames embed each proxy's own internal trace id and WILL NOT agree cross-side — the differential globs `path_prefix*` and compares the decoded trace SET, never filenames (`reference_streaming_sink_differential_framing`). **Shape precedents, NOT reusable:** `AsyncFileSink` (`internal/accesslog/writer.go:26`) opens ONE append-only file, so it is a SHAPE precedent only (`file_per_tap` opens a discrete file per stream); `internal/matcher` is a compile-then-`Evaluate` SHAPE precedent only (it evaluates a different proto, `xds.type.matcher.v3.Matcher`) — and `internal/matchpredicate` is NET-NEW (no `MatchPredicate` evaluator exists in the tree today). **10 D-TAP-* questions forwarded to the SPEC:** D-TAP-STATS, D-TAP-FILENAME, D-TAP-JSON, D-TAP-DEPRECATED, D-TAP-EMIT-TIMING, D-TAP-RESPMATCH, D-TAP-CONN, D-TAP-NOSINK/MULTISINK, D-TAP-FUZZER, and D-TAP-SINKPLACEMENT (a design pin, not empirical). **Counts UNCHANGED at this BRAINSTORM (docs-only; re-verified fresh this session against master tip `a7a7c2fb`):** stat surface **1200** / fixtures **100** / fuzzers **52** / BackendKind tail **38** / DECISIONS tail **ADR-0272** (next-free **ADR-0273**). **ANTICIPATED across the two legs (the SPEC pins):** HTTP filters **19 → 20**; fixtures **100 → 101** (`0099-http-tap-headers`, 56.1) → **102** (`0100-http-tap-bodies`, 56.2); BackendKind **38 (+0)**; **2 new packages** (`internal/headermatch`, `internal/matchpredicate`) + the filter package `internal/filter/http/tap`; **ZERO new go.mod modules** (all protos already ship in `go-control-plane/envoy v1.32.4`); ADRs **ADR-0273** (56.1) / **ADR-0274** (56.2), no parent rollup. Stat surface and fuzzer count are **SPEC probes** (D-TAP-STATS / D-TAP-FUZZER) — do NOT guess numbers. **Doc:** `docs/envoy-go/phases/56-http-tap-filter/BRAINSTORM.md`. **Next → the phase-56.1 SPEC.** **The TERMINATION SENTINEL re-checked and does NOT fire:** check (1) now prints `NOT DONE: row 56` (after this commit row 56 is the SOLE `in-progress` row — STATED EXPLICITLY so a future session is not surprised); check (2) still prints the Observability deferred sentence (`graphite`/OTLP-metrics sinks + the four tracing knobs `custom_tags`/`spawn_upstream_span`/`http_service`/force-trace); check (3) prints FIVE never-opened families (HTTP/3 + QUIC, gRPC, xDS / dynamic config, Runtime + hot restart, WASM host). The **14-task** TDD spine EXECUTED — every task got a FRESH implementer + an INDEPENDENT adversarial review; **SIX tasks needed a fix round** (recorded below as the phase's honest cost). **As built:** `Cluster.DialSink(ctx) (net.Conn, error)` — the unaccounted stats-sink dial (NO `max_connections` permit, NO `upstream_cx_*` Inc, NO `connWithGauge` wrap), sharing a NEW private `dialAndTLS` extracted from `dialPicked` (a PURE refactor — both `fmt.Errorf` strings byte-preserved); `internal/statssink/statsd_tcp.go`'s `TCPStatsdSink` — a bounded channel + writer goroutine, `delta.apply` in the WRITER (ADR-0263), `\n`-TERMINATED framing, line-aligned resume, `maxPendingBytes = 1 << 20` dropping oldest whole LINES, and a `Close` whose two-part force step (`cancel()` + `markClosedAndCloseConn()`) unwedges a blocked `Write`; `StatsdSinkConfig` becomes a TAGGED UNION and the `statsd_specifier` oneof CLOSES; TWO new reference-parity parse-time rejects (node-required; unknown-cluster); `statsdrecv` gains a TCP STREAM listener with `ConnCount()`/`UnparsedCount()`; `internal/statssink` still does NOT import `internal/cluster`. **THE HEADLINE FINDING — the deliberate-break protocol REFUTED THE PLAN'S OWN PREDICTION.** The controller performed all four §8.2 breaks itself. **(a)** redial-per-flush → `subject ConnCount = 6, want exactly 1`. **(b)** `\n`-SEPARATED framing FAILED, but NOT at the assertion the PLAN named — it aborted EARLIER, inside `pollSubset`: `timed out waiting for COUNTER subset delta-SUM == 7 (sdpfx.cluster.c_backend.upstream_rq_total=0(ok=true) …)`, so `AssertStats` was never reached and `UnparsedCount` was never evaluated. **The PLAN's premise is INVERTED:** it feared the counter-subset would pass VACUOUSLY (only the two flush-boundary names corrupt, the SAME two every flush by the stable registry walk) and added `UnparsedCount()` as the order-independent signal — but in `0098`'s ACTUAL walk order `sdpfx.cluster.c_backend.upstream_rq_total` IS one of those corrupted names, so the subset assertion is LIVE and MASKS `UnparsedCount`. **(b2)** because (b) left `UnparsedCount == 0` UNPROVEN, the controller added an isolating break — emit one unaccountable `sdpfx.bogus.timer:1|ms` line per flush, leaving every `subsetName`'s `|c` delta untouched → `subject UnparsedCount = 6, want 0` — THAT is what proves the assertion live; `UnparsedCount()` is RETAINED (the ordering that saves the subset here is an accident of the registry walk, not a guarantee — the PLAN's hazard is real in general even though its direction was wrong here). **(c)** ABSOLUTE counters → all three `|c` delta-SUMs `= 21, want 7`, with the barrier-masking proof the PLAN demanded as an extra obligation: with (c) STILL applied, deleting `awaitFurtherFlushes` makes the fixture PASS, restoring it FAILS again (the stability barrier is exactly what makes an absolute-vs-delta emit distinguishable). **(d)** `cl.Dial(ctx)` instead of `cl.DialSink(ctx)` → `subject sdpfx.cluster.c_statsd.upstream_cx_total = 1, want 0`, conclusively closing the Task-10 hand-smoke-test's inferential gap. **The SIX review fix-rounds, each a real defect the per-task review caught:** **T2** `TestTCPDiscardsIncompleteTrailingLineAtEOF`'s headline `DeltaSum("partial.li")` could NOT fail either way (no `|`, so `ingestLine` rejected it structurally even if wrongly ingested) — fixed by truncating a WELL-FORMED `partial.line:9|c`; also closed an `acceptLoop`/`Close` TOCTOU that orphaned a conn + leaked its `streamLoop` goroutine; **T6** a stale `//nolint:unused` on `dropConn`, which Task 6's own `flush` had just wired in; **T7** assertions (ii)–(v) of `TestTCPStatsdSink_LineAlignedResume` all used `t.Fatalf`, so (ii) aborted the test and (iii)/(iv)/(v) were UNREACHABLE dead code (the no-duplication/no-loss properties had never been PROVEN by any break) — now `Errorf`, each break fires its named assertion; **T8** the "ends on a line boundary" assertion was DEAD in both tests (`dropOldestLines` only cuts the FRONT, so the tail is intact by construction) — replaced with the property that protects the wire: the result is a suffix of `p` beginning at a `'\n'` boundary with `len <= capBytes` and every retained line a well-formed `name:value|type` line; **T9** the PLAN's third break (drop `if s.closed` from `redial`) was VACUOUS (`run`'s deferred `markClosedAndCloseConn` closes the racing conn either way, and the test released the dial gate before `Close` could latch `closed`) — fixed to assert `len(conn.written()) == 0` (a conn dialed after `Close` latched is never WRITTEN to) behind a `waitClosedLatched` poll (break now fires 5/5); **T13** `BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT.md` carried two contradictory present-tense `tcp_cluster_name` claims (the phase-48 block still called it an active strict-reject) plus a stale "99-dir differential" and two off-by-one ADR line citations. **Verification:** `0098` passes in isolation and the FULL 100-fixture differential suite is green; full-package `-race` on `internal/statssink` and `internal/cluster` clean; a hand smoke test of the real `main.go` boot showed one `ACCEPTED`, `\n`-terminated lines at 0.5 s cadence, and live `cluster.c_statsd.upstream_cx_total: 0` / `upstream_cx_active: 0`; the final whole-branch adversarial review returned **ZERO Critical and ZERO Important** findings. **Coverage boundaries (named as boundaries, not gaps):** the line-aligned resume is UNIT-proven, not differentially proven (the `0098` receiver never dies); cross-side `ConnCount` equality is infeasible BY THE HISTOGRAM BOUNDARY (the reference opens a second `|ms`-only worker-timer conn envoy-go can never open — reference `connCount=2`, `unparsed=68`, both RECORDED not asserted); the emitted line SET differs structurally (the reference emits only USED stats) so only NAMED SUBSETS are asserted; `membership_total == 1` is asserted but not targeted by any deliberate break; when CDS lands, the static-cluster reject must move to the cluster manager. **Eight carried Minors, all triaged CARRY by the final review:** `dialAndTLS` declared after `dialPicked`; a 100 ms sleep in the EOF-discard test; no TCP-path tagged-line test; a deliberately-redundant trap-2 regression test; `logDrop`'s shared rate-limit bucket; `dropOldestLines` would panic on a negative cap (unreachable); two tests each pay `closeDrainGrace`; `0098`'s `d.ref`/`d.subj` written without `d.mu` (verbatim from `0092`, sequential-safe via the runner's join). **Counts re-derived at IMPL exit (not copied):** stat surface **1200 → 1200 (+0)** / fixtures **99 → 100** (`0098-stats-sink-statsd-tcp`) / fuzzers **52 (+0)** / BackendKind tail **38 (+0)** / DECISIONS **ADR-0271 → ADR-0272** (next-free **ADR-0273**); - **prior active-phase:** `phase 55 (stats-sink-statsd-tcp) PLAN done` (2026-07-09) — the EIGHTH Observability-family row's PLAN LANDED (lifecycle-state 2 → 3; docs-only, worktree `.worktrees/phase-55-plan`, branch `phase-55-stats-sink-statsd-tcp-plan`, FF-merged to master + pushed per `feedback_push_to_origin`). **Row 55 STAYS `in-progress`.** `superpowers:writing-plans` decomposed SPEC §10 into a **14-task** bite-sized TDD spine; the **plan-document-reviewer gate** ran as TWO adversarial subagent reviews (one against the as-built source, one against the SPEC + the memory bank) and **caught TWO BLOCKERs plus four line-citation drifts, all fixed in the landed document**. **THE FIVE §12 D-QUESTIONS ARE RESOLVED, each from source evidence rather than prose.** **D-TCP-CLOSE (LOAD-BEARING) → a `sync.Mutex` guarding `{conn, closed}`**, NOT an `atomic.Pointer[net.Conn]` (a pointer-to-interface-value that still needs a separate closed flag) and NOT a ctx-watching closer goroutine (which needs the same guarded field anyway): the mutex guards the FIELD, never the I/O — the writer copies the conn out under the lock and does the blocking `Write` UNLOCKED, so `Close` can hard-close it from another goroutine, which unwedges the parked `Write` with an error, while the `closed` flag (read under the same lock in `redial`) stops the writer leaking a freshly-dialed conn; `cancel()` survives but its job narrows to aborting an in-flight `DialContext`. `-race`-PROVEN at IMPL Task 9 via a `blockingConn` double, with the deliberate break 'drop `markClosedAndCloseConn` from the force branch ⇒ the test MUST TIME OUT' as the standing evidence that `cancel()` alone cannot interrupt a raw `net.Conn`. **D-TCP-CONFIG-SHAPE → a TAGGED UNION** (`TCPClusterName string` added to `StatsdSinkConfig`), not a fourth `Bootstrap.StatsdTCPSinkConfigs` slice: the three landed slices are separated by distinct TypeURLs, whereas `address`/`tcp_cluster_name` are two arms of ONE oneof inside ONE TypeURL, and a second slice would destroy each slice's documented 'in declaration order' property for interleaved UDP/TCP sinks. **D-TCP-PENDING-CAP → `maxPendingBytes = 1 << 20` (1 MiB), dropping whole LINES** (not whole SNAPSHOTS — `pending` is a flat `\n`-terminated buffer with NO snapshot delimiters, and a partial line on the wire would corrupt the receiver's stream parse): envoy-go's `snapshot()` walks the WHOLE frozen registry (~1200 stats × ~50 B ≈ 60 KB/flush, vs the reference's used-only emission), so 1 MiB retains ≈16 snapshots — the same order as `MetricsServiceSink`'s cap-8 channel — and 1 MiB is already the tree's in-process buffer bound (ADR-0076). **D-TCP-NODE-PLACEMENT → PARSE-TIME, and the SPEC's shorthand CORRECTED:** `result.Proto` is the `*bootstrap.Bootstrap` WRAPPER's field (`bootstrap.go:351`), reachable inside `parseStatsdSinkConfig` because `Load` builds `&Bootstrap{Proto: bs}` at `:467` before `parseStatsSinks` runs at `:471` — there is **NO `Proto` field on `StatsdSinkConfig`** and none must be added. Parse-time placement gets the landed `FuzzStatsdSinkConfigParse` + the `TestStatsdSink_Rejects` table for free. **The unknown-cluster reject ALSO moves to parse time** (against `result.Proto.GetStaticResources().GetClusters()`), a deliberate and VERIFIED-SAFE deviation from SPEC §6's 'boot fatal' phrasing: envoy-go has NO CDS (`internal/xds/doc.go` is a phase-00 placeholder; `Manager` exposes no dynamic cluster Add), so static clusters ARE the complete set and parse-time IS boot time; crucially, per `reference_differential_fixture_dispatch_constraint` a boot-reject fixture cannot share `0098`'s directory, so a `main.go`-only check would have shipped **UNTESTED**. `main.go` keeps a defensive `cm.Get` fatal + a 'when CDS lands, move this' marker. **D-TCP-DIALNAME → `func (c *Cluster) DialSink(ctx context.Context) (net.Conn, error)`** — two returns, NOT `Dial`'s three (`Dial(ctx) (net.Conn, Endpoint, error)`, `cluster.go:536`; the Endpoint exists only to feed `RecordUpstreamResult` for outlier detection, which a sink never does) — sharing a NEW private `dialAndTLS(ctx, ep) (net.Conn, error)` extracted from `dialPicked`. **`dialPicked`'s `ownPermit=false` is NOT sufficient**, contrary to what its own doc comment invites: it unconditionally Incs `upstream_cx_{total,active}` (`:600-601`) and wraps in `connWithGauge{dec: connDec(release)}`, whose dec Decs the gauge AND calls `pool.releaseConn()` — underflowing a permit `DialSink` never took. `DialSink` uses `PickEndpoint()` (immediate release) and returns the BARE conn. Named for its POLICY SCOPE, not its mechanism: `DialUnaccounted` would invite any caller to dodge the permit. **THE PLAN MAKES THREE CORRECTIONS TO THE SPEC**, each verified against master tip `708c0dcc`: (i) `Cluster.Dial`'s real signature is `(net.Conn, Endpoint, error)`; (ii) **`tcp_cluster_name` appears at SIX Go sites, not one** — the SPEC names only `statsd_fuzz_test.go:47-52`, but there is a SECOND fuzz seed at `statssink_fuzz_test.go:110-115` and a table case at `bootstrap_test.go:2075-2082`, all of which Task 3 must flip or the tests will lie; (iii) `result.Proto` is the wrapper's field (above). **AND IT STRENGTHENS ONE SPEC ASSERTION THAT WOULD HAVE BEEN VACUOUS.** SPEC §8.2's deliberate break **(b)** ('emit `\n`-SEPARATED ⇒ the counter-subset lookups miss') is **NOT reliably live**: `\n`-separated framing concatenates the last line of flush N with the first of flush N+1 into `A:7|cB:1|g`, which the receiver's first-pipe-then-colon parser reads as a VALID name/value with the unknown type token `cB:1` — so exactly TWO names are lost per flush boundary, and `snapshot()`'s stable walk order makes them the SAME two names every flush. If neither falls inside the three asserted `subsetNames`, break (b) **passes vacuously** (the `reference_differential_asserter_dispatch` failure class). The PLAN therefore adds **`statsdrecv.UnparsedCount()`** (lines the receiver could not account: a structural parse failure OR an unknown metric type) and asserts it **SUBJECT-EXACT at 0** — envoy-go emits only `|c`/`|g` (ADR-0060), while the reference legitimately emits 35 `|ms` lines, so it is recorded not asserted there. Deterministic, order-independent, and it bites break (b) every time. Task 12 additionally REQUIRES the implementer to observe and RECORD whether the subset assertion also fires, rather than assume it. **Fixture `0098-stats-sink-statsd-tcp`** asserts cross-side payload parity (three `|c` delta-SUMs == 7 behind the `≥2`-further-flush stability barrier per `reference_delta_sink_differential_stability_barrier`, plus `membership_total == 1` per `reference_membership_total_vs_healthy_gauge`) and **THREE** subject-exact transport assertions (`ConnCount() == 1`; `UnparsedCount() == 0`; `sdpfx.cluster.c_statsd.upstream_cx_total == 0`), all three verified NON-VACUOUS at the review gate: `registerClusterMetrics` registers `upstream_cx_total` unconditionally for every cluster including `c_statsd`, envoy-go emits the whole registry, and `statsdrecv`'s `deltaSums[name] += 0` on a `|c` line CREATES the map entry, so `DeltaSum` returns `(0, true)` and the `cxTotalOK` guard distinguishes absent-from-zero. Both bootstraps MUST carry `node: {id, cluster}` (AMEND-TCP-NODE) — 0092's `// NO node field is needed` comment is true for UDP and FALSE for TCP. **The `reference_admin_interface_wire_name_collision` watch-item is CLOSED on the record, not silently:** it bites only TAG-HOISTING sinks (dog_statsd / metrics_service `emit_tags_as_labels`), where `ExtractTags` collapses the admin HCM and the test HCM onto one wire name differing only by tag value; plain statsd hoists no tags and emits raw dotted names, so `sdpfx.http.hcm_local.*` ≠ `sdpfx.http.admin.*` and the readiness probe cannot contaminate the subset — `0098` uses plain `DeltaSum`, NOT `DeltaSumTagged`. `reference_dynamic_stat_name_charset_guard` is confirmed INAPPLICABLE: this phase registers ZERO stats and derives NO stat name from wire bytes. **FINAL ADR-0045 SPLIT-GATE RE-CHECK: 14 tasks — the gate is TOUCHED, NOT EXCEEDED ⇒ NO SPLIT** (one subsystem, one new prod file, one ADR, one fixture; the reviewer independently judged the count HONEST, not gamed). The escape valve is re-armed with a concrete trigger: if IMPL Task 7 (line-aligned resume) or Task 9 (`-race` `Close`) forces a split past 15 tasks, STOP and re-open the gate as 55.1 (transport + rejects) / 55.2 (fixture + breaks). **Counts UNCHANGED at this PLAN (docs-only; re-verified fresh this session against master tip `708c0dcc`):** stat surface **1200** / fixtures **99** / fuzzers **52** / BackendKind tail **38** / DECISIONS tail **ADR-0271** (next-free **ADR-0272**). Anticipated at IMPL: fixtures **100**, everything else **+0**. **Doc:** `docs/envoy-go/phases/55-stats-sink-statsd-tcp/PLAN.md`. **Next → the phase-55 IMPL** (`superpowers:subagent-driven-development` in a FRESH worktree `.worktrees/phase-55-impl`; subagents commit LOCALLY only per `feedback_subagents_no_push`; the CONTROLLER performs Task 12's four deliberate breaks ITSELF — including break (c)'s barrier-masking proof and break (b)'s subset-vacuity observation — squashes at stage-close, runs the six gates, and pushes. The merge gate runs a **FULL-package `-race`** on `internal/statssink`, `internal/cluster`, AND `test/differential` per `reference_full_suite_race_after_background_mutator`: `TCPStatsdSink` is the statsd sinks' FIRST background mutator).

- **prior active-phase:** `phase 55 (stats-sink-statsd-tcp) SPEC done` (2026-07-08) — the EIGHTH Observability-family row's SPEC LANDED (lifecycle-state 1 → 2; docs-only, worktree `.worktrees/phase-55-spec`, branch `phase-55-stats-sink-statsd-tcp-spec`, FF-merged to master + pushed per `feedback_push_to_origin`). The §11 D-TCP-* empirical pins were EXECUTED IN-SESSION against `envoyproxy/envoy:contrib-v1.37.2` (ADR-0004/ADR-0227) on a Docker bridge network (`reference_docker_probe_bridge_network`), `--concurrency 1`, with a driver-owned byte-logging TCP receiver on the bridge (sidestepping the host-gateway question for the probe itself). **FOUR pins CONFIRMED, THREE AMENDED — and the most consequential amendment REFUTES THE STATED PREMISE OF A USER DECISION.** **AMEND-TCP-RESUME (LOAD-BEARING):** the BRAINSTORM told the human that the reference 'buffers into the conn and drops the buffer on connection failure, it does not replay'. It does the OPPOSITE. Over a 30-second receiver outage the reference ACCUMULATED every unwritten flush snapshot and delivered ALL of them on reconnect — 45 snapshots, `server.uptime` running 5..32+ with no gap; in the short-outage arm ONE 37044-byte write carried EIGHT concatenated snapshots (`uptime` 6..13) whose `downstream_rq_total` deltas were `[0,4,0,0,0,0,0,0]`, SUM==4 == exactly the requests driven during the outage (cross-receiver total 7 == K). **Nothing lost, nothing duplicated.** Q3's dichotomy (replay double-counts vs drop loses) was therefore a FALSE DICHOTOMY: because `conn.Write` returns `n`, a third option exists — retain the unwritten suffix REALIGNED to the last complete line boundary `<= n` (`s.pending = s.pending[bytes.LastIndexByte(s.pending[:n], '\n')+1:]`), so complete lines that landed are NEVER re-sent while the ONE straddling line — which the dead connection's stream parser discards at EOF — is re-sent WHOLE. The human, shown the evidence, **RE-DECIDED Q3 → bounded accumulate + line-aligned resume**; envoy-go BOUNDS `pending` (`maxPendingBytes`, drop-oldest + rate-limited log) where the reference is effectively unbounded — a DELIBERATE, documented departure consistent with `MetricsServiceSink`'s cap-8 drop-newest posture. **AMEND-TCP-CXSTATS (refutes the Q2 anticipation; ACTIVATES the BRAINSTORM's pre-authorized Q2 option (c)):** the reference's stats cluster reports `upstream_cx_total: 0` and `upstream_cx_active: 0` while `upstream_cx_tx_bytes_total: 16511` moves (a stat NAME envoy-go does not register anywhere), and with `circuit_breakers.thresholds[].max_connections: 0` on the stats cluster it STILL connects and STILL flushes (`circuit_breakers.default.cx_open: 0`, `upstream_cx_overflow: 0`) — the statsd connection bypasses conn-pool accounting AND the `max_connections` permit. So `Cluster.Dial` (which Incs both counters, takes a permit, and wraps in `connWithGauge`) is NOT reusable: envoy-go gains a narrow, exported, stats-scoped **`Cluster.DialSink(ctx)`** — LB pick + `connect_timeout` + upstream TLS, NO permit, NO `upstream_cx_*`, no wrapper. The BRAINSTORM pre-authorized exactly this branch ('Defer unless D-TCP-CXSTATS shows the reference exempts it'), so no new dialogue was needed. **AMEND-TCP-NODE (NEW — anticipated by nothing):** the reference REFUSES TO BOOT a `tcp_cluster_name` statsd sink unless BOTH `node.id` and `node.cluster` are set (`tcp statsd: node 'id' and 'cluster' are required.…`; either field alone fails with the identical message), while the UDP `address` sink boots with NO node at all (control probe) — a TCP-SPECIFIC byte-stable reject (ADR-0080) envoy-go must add; `bs.Proto.GetNode()` is already read at `main.go:155` (`minNode`) so the data is in hand. **AMEND-TCP-CONNCOUNT:** the reference opens **1** connection with zero traffic and **2** under load; conn #2 carries ONLY `|ms` timer lines (35 of them, 5 worker timer names) — it is the worker-thread sink, connecting lazily on its first histogram emission. envoy-go has NO histograms (ADR-0060) and can never open it ⇒ a cross-side `ConnCount` equality is infeasible **by the histogram boundary**, a STRUCTURAL reason rather than the thread-model *uncertainty* Q4 hedged against. Q4's subject-exact `ConnCount() == 1` STANDS, on firmer ground. **AMEND-TCP-USEDONLY (NEW):** the reference emits only USED stats — `cluster.c_statsd.upstream_cx_total` sits at `0` in `/stats` yet appears in NO emitted line, whereas `membership_change:0|c` (incremented once, now delta 0) IS emitted every flush — so the emitted line SET differs cross-side by construction; `0098` asserts NAMED SUBSETS only (as `0092` already does) and `upstream_cx_total == 0` is asserted SUBJECT-side. **CONFIRMED:** D-TCP-LINE (every line `\n`-TERMINATED **including the last**, no `\n\n`; ONE `conn.Write` per periodic flush — 99 lines / ~4650 bytes; write granularity is NOT observable to a line-parsing stream receiver and is not asserted); D-TCP-DELTA (COUNTER `|c` per-flush delta — `downstream_rq_total` `[2,5,0,0,0,0]` SUM==7 over K=7 — GAUGE `|g` absolute; **zero-delta counters are STILL emitted**, matching `deltaState.apply`); D-TCP-STATS (ZERO statsd-sink self-stats); D-TCP-REJECT (unknown `tcp_cluster_name` → boot reject `tcp statsd: unknown cluster 'c_nonexistent'` = **reference PARITY, not an envoy-go-strict departure**, correcting the BRAINSTORM §5.2; `statsd_specifier` absent → PGV `field: "statsd_specifier", reason: is required`; an existing-but-UNREACHABLE cluster BOOTS and the sink retries). Incidental: a UDP `address` with a hostname is rejected (`malformed IP address: recv55`) — the statsd UDP `socket_address` must be an IP literal, matching envoy-go's existing doc-comment. **The `statsdrecv` stream-parser hazard is now MEASURED, not hypothetical:** the first post-reconnect write (~200 KB) arrived in `recv()` chunks capped at 65536 bytes, splitting lines mid-token — the TCP listener MUST read line-at-a-time through `bufio` with a carried remainder and discard an incomplete trailing line at EOF (which §3.5's no-duplication argument depends on), per `reference_line_parser_extension_delimiter_reuse`. `0098-stats-sink-statsd-tcp` gains a FOURTH deliberate break (use `Cluster.Dial` instead of `DialSink` → subject `c_statsd.upstream_cx_total` becomes 1) plus the subject-exact `upstream_cx_total == 0` assertion; the line-aligned resume is UNIT-proven (the fixture's receiver never dies). Both bootstraps MUST now carry `node: {id, cluster}` or the reference will not boot. **ADR-0045 re-check:** the amendments grew the envelope ~180–230 → **~250–330 prod LoC** (`statsd_tcp.go` ~200 / `DialSink` ~40 / bootstrap ~40 / `main.go` ~20); still ONE subsystem, ONE new file, ONE ADR, ONE fixture ⇒ **NO SPLIT**, escape valve re-armed if the PLAN exceeds ~14 tasks. **`TCPStatsdSink` is the statsd sinks' FIRST background mutator** ⇒ the IMPL merge gate runs a FULL-package `-race` on `internal/statssink`, `internal/cluster`, and `test/differential` (`reference_full_suite_race_after_background_mutator`). Counts UNCHANGED at this SPEC (docs-only; re-verified fresh against master tip `2bfa3db6`): stat surface **1200** / fixtures **99** / fuzzers **52** / BackendKind tail **38** / DECISIONS tail **ADR-0271** (next-free **ADR-0272**). §13 anchors the **ADR-0272 §Context DRAFT** (§Decision/§Consequences land at the IMPL per ADR-0044). **Doc:** `docs/envoy-go/phases/55-stats-sink-statsd-tcp/SPEC.md`. **Next → the phase-55 PLAN** (`superpowers:writing-plans` — decompose SPEC §10's ~12–14 tasks into a bite-sized TDD spine; resolve the FIVE §12 D-questions: **D-TCP-CLOSE** [the `-race`-proven mechanism by which `Close` unwedges a blocked `Write` on the writer-goroutine-owned conn — `cancel()` cannot interrupt a raw `net.Conn`], **D-TCP-CONFIG-SHAPE**, and the three NEW ones **D-TCP-PENDING-CAP** / **D-TCP-NODE-PLACEMENT** / **D-TCP-DIALNAME**; FINAL ADR-0045 split-gate re-check).

- **prior active-phase:** `phase 55 (stats-sink-statsd-tcp) BRAINSTORM done` (2026-07-08) — the EIGHTH Observability-family row CHARTERED (lifecycle-state 0 → 1; docs-only, worktree `.worktrees/phase-55-brainstorm`, branch `phase-55-stats-sink-statsd-tcp-brainstorm`, FF-merged to master + pushed per `feedback_push_to_origin`). The loop RE-OPENED after the Load-balancing family closed at phase 54; the deferred candidate picked is the plain-statsd **`tcp_cluster_name` transport**, lifting the phase-48 STRICT-REJECT at `internal/bootstrap/bootstrap.go:567-568` into an accept-and-honor path — the exact shape phase 50 used to lift phase-49's `max_bytes_per_datagram` reject. **Row 55 registered `in-progress` AT this BRAINSTORM commit** (the ROADMAP §Schema invariant). **5-question user dialogue:** **(Q1 writer shape)** BOUNDED CHANNEL + WRITER GOROUTINE (the `MetricsServiceSink`/ADR-0262 shape) — phase 48's SYNCHRONOUS `Submit` was explicitly licensed by a property TCP does not have (`SPEC-48.md:78`: *"a UDP `Write` is non-blocking fire-and-forget … a UDP datagram never blocks on a peer"*), and since the `Flusher` calls `Submit` SERIALLY across all sinks from its single goroutine, a blocking TCP sink would starve every sibling sink; accepted cost — the statsd sinks' FIRST background mutator, so `reference_full_suite_race_after_background_mutator` governs the merge gate (FULL-package `-race`, not a `-run`-subset). **(Q2 dial primitive)** `Cluster.Dial(ctx)` (`internal/cluster/cluster.go:536`), the landed general primitive already used by `tcp_proxy`/`redis_proxy`/`thrift_proxy` and the `grpcclient` dial-callback — free LB endpoint pick + `connect_timeout` + upstream TLS + the `max_connections` permit + `upstream_cx_total`/`upstream_cx_active` accounting; ACCEPTED CONSEQUENCE: a stable SELF-REFERENCE (the sink reports, over its own connection, the `cx` counters that connection produced), EXPECTED to match the reference — which routes its own `TcpStatsdSink` through its cluster manager — pinned by D-TCP-CXSTATS and deliberately UNASSERTED in the differential. Verified leak-free at `cluster.go:359-365`: `connWithGauge.Close()` runs one `sync.Once` closure that Decs the gauge, releases the LB pick, AND releases the `max_connections` permit, so a reconnect loop leaks neither. **(Q3 write-error policy)** DROP THE BATCH, RECONNECT LAZILY, **NEVER REPLAY** — the ONE place the `MetricsServiceSink` precedent does NOT transfer: that sink reopens and re-sends the whole batch on a `Send` error, safe because gRPC messages are atomic units, but a raw TCP `conn.Write` can return `0 < n < len`, so a prefix of the batch's statsd lines already landed, and replaying them double-counts a receiver that SUMS `|c` deltas; the dropped batch's increments are lost (already latched by `delta.apply`) — accepted, matching statsd's lossy-by-design contract and the phase-48 UDP sink's own drop-on-write-error posture. Every write additionally carries a `SetWriteDeadline` so a wedged peer cannot stall the writer unboundedly. **(Q4 differential proof shape)** PAYLOAD PARITY CROSS-SIDE + SUBJECT-EXACT `ConnCount() == 1` — payload parity ALONE would pass even if the sink redialed on EVERY flush, leaving the phase's single most important new behavior (a long-lived connection) differentially unproven; but a CROSS-SIDE conn-count assertion bets on Envoy's thread-local statsd sink yielding exactly 1 under `--concurrency 1`, so the reference's count is RECORDED-but-UNASSERTED pending D-TCP-CONNCOUNT (the `reference_max_connections_soft_breaker` subject-exact + cross-side-robust precedent). **(Q5 envelope)** SINGLE FLAT ROW, **ADR-0272** (~180–230 prod LoC; both candidate splits rejected with reasons — a `transport/differential` split would strand a LIFTED strict-reject with no differential proof, the exact shape ADR-0080 exists to prevent; a `sink/reconnect-hardening` split would ship a sink that mishandles a peer restart to defer ~15 LoC; ADR-0045 escape-valve UNCONSUMED). **SELF-ANSWERED from the proto (not assumed, not probed):** `StatsdSink`'s `statsd_specifier` oneof has EXACTLY TWO arms (`go-control-plane/envoy@v1.37.0` `config/metrics/v3/stats.pb.go:571-577,675-691`), so lifting `tcp_cluster_name` **CLOSES** the oneof — discharging `reference_strict_reject_sibling_typeurl_gap` at the oneof level (the independent TypeURL-level `parseStatsSinks` dispatch is unchanged and still rejects unknown sink TypeURLs); and `DogStatsdSink`'s oneof has ONLY the `address` member (`stats.pb.go:702-703`), so there is no dog_statsd analog of this row. **The ordering constraint survives the lift with INVERTED meaning:** `bootstrap.go:556-561` notes `GetAddress()` returns nil for BOTH a missing oneof AND a `tcp_cluster_name` arm, which is why the reject ran FIRST; after the lift the `tcp_cluster_name` arm must be DISPATCHED before the nil-`address` reject fires, or a valid TCP config would be rejected as "missing `statsd_specifier`" — a regression test must pin this. **Design shape:** `internal/statssink` will NOT import `internal/cluster`; `NewTCPStatsdSink` takes a `func(context.Context) (net.Conn, error)` dial seam that `main.go` closes over (`cm.Get(name)` then `cl.Dial(ctx)`, re-looked-up per dial as `grpcclient.go:137` does) — the landed `metricsClient`-interface decoupling trick, reduced to a `func` because the seam has one method and no shared state. `StatsdSink` (UDP), `DogStatsdSink`, `udpWriter`, `Flusher`, `Sink`, `deltaState`, and `emitStatsdLines` are ALL untouched; delta is applied in the WRITER goroutine (not `Submit`) per ADR-0263 so an enqueue-drop never latches. **Sharpest carried lesson:** `reference_line_parser_extension_delimiter_reuse` — `statsdrecv.ingest` splits a UDP DATAGRAM on `\n`, where every line is complete by construction; a TCP `Read` carries no such guarantee, so the new TCP listener must read line-at-a-time through `bufio` with a carried remainder and discard an incomplete trailing line at EOF, and the PLAN task must trace a concrete split-read example byte-for-byte rather than describe the grammar. **Anticipated counts:** stat surface **1200** (+0 — the `upstream_cx_*` self-reference is a new stat INSTANCE, not a new NAME) / fixtures **99 → 100** (`0098-stats-sink-statsd-tcp`) / fuzzers **52** (+0 — the landed `FuzzStatsdSinkConfigParse` already carries a `tcp_cluster_name` seed whose expected outcome merely flips from reject to accept; reconcile against `grep -c '^func Fuzz'` per `reference_fuzzer_count_docs_drift`) / BackendKind **38** (+0, driver-owned TCP receiver) / DECISIONS tail **ADR-0271** (next-free **ADR-0272**); ZERO new packages, ZERO new go.mod modules. Counts UNCHANGED at this BRAINSTORM (docs-only; re-verified fresh this session against master tip `fa0fd6b3`). **Doc:** `docs/envoy-go/phases/55-stats-sink-statsd-tcp/BRAINSTORM.md`. **Next → the phase-55 SPEC** (execute the §10 D-TCP-LINE / D-TCP-DELTA / D-TCP-CONNCOUNT / D-TCP-CXSTATS / D-TCP-RECONNECT / D-TCP-REJECT live pins IN-SESSION against `envoyproxy/envoy:contrib-v1.37.2` per ADR-0004/ADR-0227 via `reference_docker_probe_bridge_network`; D-TCP-LINE is LOAD-BEARING and adopts the reference's framing verbatim per `reference_wire_format_both_sides_see_same_bytes`; anchor the ADR-0272 §Context draft; the D-TCP-CLOSE and D-TCP-CONFIG-SHAPE pins defer to the PLAN).

The 11-task TDD spine EXECUTED: **AMEND-PT1** — the reference INTEGER-TRUNCATES the threshold, so `clusterHealth.panicThreshold float64` (fraction) → `panicThresholdPercent int` (D-PT-STORE), `parsePanicThreshold` FLOORS (`int(math.Floor(value))`), `inPanic` compares via the ULP-safe integer cross-multiply `100*availableCount < panicThresholdPercent*total` (strict `<`; agrees with the old float form at every integer threshold, corrects the `60.9%`@`60%` over-panic); **AMEND-PT4** — a STANDALONE unconditional `validatePanicThresholdRange` out-of-range `[0,100]` reject early in `buildCluster` (byte-stable ADR-0080; fires for a plain no-HC cluster the three health-guarded `parsePanicThreshold` sites never reach); **AMEND-PT3** — the `locality.go` child-local-panic RETROFIT (per-locality children built against a panic-DISABLED `tierHealth` view; `tierHealth` relocated to `health.go`, `priorityLeafFactory`→`healthLeafFactory` relocated to `loadbalancer.go` and SHARED by locality+priority; the flat fallback child keeps shared panic-enabled health; `subsetLB` UNCHANGED — the crux asymmetry, a subset panics per-subset — with a degraded-subset-still-flattens regression); **AMEND-PT2** — the `lb_healthy_panic` double-increment fix (REMOVE the redundant outer `panicInc()` at `localityWeightedLB.Pick`; the shared-health flat child's own `panicGate` provides the single increment, `2N → N`); proven by the **`0097-lb-panic-threshold`** three-arm cross-side differential (A `{value:80}` PANICS all-5-hosts / B absent-50% no-panic / C `{value:60.9}` floor-60 no-panic — the integer-truncation discriminator) — 3 deliberate breaks bite `-count=1`, 20/20 flake-free, `-race` clean; the AMEND-PT2/PT3 unit assertions scratch-revert-proven live by the controller. ADR-0271 body + BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT panic-construct delta landed. Six gates GREEN + full **99**-dir differential green (+ full-package `-race` on `internal/cluster` and `test/differential`). Counts: fixtures **98 → 99**, DECISIONS tail **ADR-0270 → ADR-0271**, stat surface **1200 (+0)** (the family's FIFTH zero-stat-delta phase — a VALUE correction on `lb_healthy_panic`), fuzzers **52 (+0)**, BackendKind **38 (+0)**, ZERO new packages/go.mod deps/`Pick` params/`Endpoint` dimension. **ROW 54 `done` — the Load-balancing family CLOSES** (the FOURTH family to close, after HTTP-filters @ 25.3 / Network-filters @ 33 / Upstream-robustness @ 43). **Next → the phase-55 (`stats-sink-statsd-tcp`) BRAINSTORM** — the loop RE-OPENED after the Load-balancing family closed; the next deferred candidate picked is the plain-statsd `tcp_cluster_name` transport (the EIGHTH Observability-family row), lifting the phase-48 strict-reject at `internal/bootstrap/bootstrap.go:567` exactly as phase 50 lifted phase-49's `max_bytes_per_datagram` reject. The FOUR §12 PLAN/IMPL design questions are RESOLVED: **D-PT-STORE** → an INTEGER field `clusterHealth.panicThresholdPercent int` (fraction `float64` → floored integer percent; `newClusterHealth`/`parsePanicThreshold` retype `float64→int`; `int` not `uint32` so `inPanic`'s cross-multiply `100*availableCount < panicThresholdPercent*total` is zero-conversion arithmetic); **D-PT-REJECT-PLACEMENT** → a STANDALONE `validatePanicThresholdRange(c, name)` helper (in `health.go`) called UNCONDITIONALLY early in `buildCluster` right after `extractEndpoints` (BEFORE the health parse + LB switch) — REQUIRED because all three `parsePanicThreshold` sites (`manager.go:461/501/536`) are health-guarded, so folding the range check there would miss a plain no-HC cluster the reference still rejects; **D-PT-TIERHEALTH-HOME** → relocate `tierHealth` to `health.go` (its 2nd consumer) + rename `priorityLeafFactory → healthLeafFactory` (relocated to `loadbalancer.go`, SHARED by locality+priority — a `priority`-specific name on a `localityWeightedLB` param would mislead) + the health-parameterized signature change to `newLocalityWeightedLB(WithRNG)` + the `manager.go:513-515` closure (LOAD-BEARING); **D-PT-UNIT-VS-DIFF** → AMEND-PT2/PT3 stay UNIT-proven against the D-PT1(iii)/D-PT4 pinned values (each new unit assertion scratch-revert-proven live), NO locality/subset differential dir. - **prior active-phase:** `phase 54 (load-balancer-panic-threshold) SPEC done` (2026-07-08) — the EIGHTH and FINAL Load-balancing-family row's SPEC LANDED (lifecycle-state 1 → 2; docs-only, worktree `.worktrees/phase-54-spec`, branch `phase-54-load-balancer-panic-threshold-spec`). The §11 D-PT1..D-PT6 empirical pins were EXECUTED IN-SESSION live against `envoyproxy/envoy:contrib-v1.37.2` (ADR-0227) on a Docker bridge network (`reference_docker_probe_bridge_network`), `--concurrency 1`, per-host `rq_total` ground truth from `/clusters`. **The single most consequential finding — NOT anticipated by the BRAINSTORM — is AMEND-PT1: the reference INTEGER-TRUNCATES (floors) the configured threshold to a whole percent before comparing** (`100.0 × healthy / total < floor(threshold_percent)`), so envoy-go's current float-fraction compare (`available/total < value/100`) OVER-PANICS at every non-integer threshold (probed: `60.9%` threshold at `60%` healthy → reference does NOT panic, envoy-go does; `66.7`/`66.5`/`66` at `66.67%` → no panic, `67` → panic — all fit `floor`, refuting round-half-up) → the fix floors the value + compares via an INTEGER CROSS-MULTIPLY (`100·available < floor(value)·total`, strict `<`, the ULP-safe exact mirror per `reference_percent_cap_cross_multiply`; agrees with the old form at the integer thresholds every prior test used, so the gap lay dormant). **THREE further evidence-driven corrections/confirmations:** (AMEND-PT2, D-PT1(iii)) the reference increments `lb_healthy_panic` EXACTLY ONCE per pick on BOTH flat AND locality-weighted panic (delta==N over N requests) → the double-increment fix is REFINED to simply REMOVE the redundant outer `panicInc()` at `locality.go:144` (the shared-health flat child's own `panicGate` provides the single increment — cleaner + lower-risk than the BRAINSTORM's anticipated nil-health flat child, valid because localityWeightedLB's bypass condition is identical-by-construction to the flat child's own `inPanic`; the deliberate contrast with `priorityLB`/ADR-0270, and it preserves the zero-total-weight fallback a nil-health child would have changed); (AMEND-PT3, D-PT4) the reference does NOT locally panic within a degraded locality (a locality at 40% healthy kept its unhealthy hosts at ZERO, its share split only across its 2 healthy hosts per the effective-weight formula) → the `locality.go` child-local-panic RETROFIT is CONFIRMED needed (build per-locality children against panic-disabled `tierHealth` views, ADR-0270's primitive as its 2nd consumer; the flat fallback child stays shared/panic-enabled so it alone does the single increment), AND the crux asymmetry is pinned: a `lb_subset_config` cluster DOES panic per-subset (a degraded subset flattened — all its hosts incl unhealthy served, delta==N) → `subsetLB`'s shared-health children are already faithful → NO subset change; (AMEND-PT4, D-PT3) the reference PGV-REJECTS `healthy_panic_threshold.value` outside `[0,100]` at boot (`{value:150}`/`{value:-10}` → `PercentValidationError.Value: value must be inside range [0, 100]`; `{value:0}` → OK) → envoy-go adds a byte-stable range reject (ADR-0080). AMEND-PT5 (D-PT2): presence semantics (value 0 disables, absent=50%, explicit 50) CONFIRMED — envoy-go already matches. AMEND-PT6 (D-PT5/D-PT6): stat surface CONFIRMED ZERO (only `lb_healthy_panic` + the pre-existing, out-of-scope `lb_subsets_fallback_panic`); envelope ~150–260 prod LoC / ~9–11 tasks, NO split, NO fuzzer. **Fixture `0097-lb-panic-threshold` refined to THREE arms** (a SPEC enhancement over the Q2 two-cluster shape, justified by AMEND-PT1 and DETERMINISTIC — an integer cross-multiply, NOT the float-parity boundary risk the human declined for a one-cluster shape): cluster A `{value:80}` PANICS (all 5 hosts incl the 2 unhealthy `>0` offset-invariant, `lb_healthy_panic == N_A`), cluster B ABSENT/50% NO panic (2 unhealthy `==0`, counter `==0`), cluster C `{value:60.9}` NO panic (the integer-truncation discriminator — FAILS pre-fix, PASSES post-fix), all at the SAME 2-of-5 degradation (60% healthy); three `-count=1` deliberate breaks (a: hardcode 50 → A stops panicking; b: skip degradation → A's panic asserts fail; c: revert the floor → C panics). The double-increment + retrofit corrections are UNIT-proven against the D-PT1(iii)/D-PT4 pinned values (NO locality/subset differential dir — honoring the plain-cluster Q2 scope). Anticipated **1 ADR (ADR-0271)** — §Context DRAFT anchored at SPEC §13, §Decision/§Consequences at IMPL per ADR-0044. `go mod tidy` no-op (ZERO new dep). - **prior active-phase:** `phase 53 (load-balancer-priority) IMPL done` (2026-07-07) — the SEVENTH Load-balancing-family row BUILT end-to-end (subagent-driven in the worktree `.worktrees/phase-53-impl`, branch `phase-53-load-balancer-priority-impl`, squash-merged to master, lifecycle-state 3 → 4 phase-done); **ANCHORS ADR-0270** (§Decision/§Consequences landed IN-PLACE per ADR-0044, §Context promoted from SPEC §13). All **11 PLAN tasks** landed exactly per the TDD spine (no fold/split from the SPEC §10 indicative numbering). A new `internal/cluster/priority.go` `priorityLB` WRAPPER `loadBalancer` (the phase-52 `localityWeightedLB` structural precedent, generalized from a flat weighted draw across peer localities to a CASCADING WATERFALL across ordered tiers) groups a cluster's endpoints by `LocalityLbEndpoints.priority` tier and, on every `Pick`, computes each tier's effective capacity from its live per-host health fraction scaled by the EXISTING `overprovisioning_factor` (a phase-52 dependency REUSED verbatim, threading `(opf uint32, hasOPF bool)` per the D-LW-OPF0 precedent), cascades load across tiers via the CORRECTED (not naive-recursive) AMEND-P4 formula (each tier's load capped by its own capacity against the REMAINING budget — the two readings coincide at exactly 2 tiers but diverge at 3+), and — on the genuinely NEW cluster-wide capacity-SHORTFALL condition `Σ_i min(100, overprovisioning × healthy_fraction_i) < 100` (AMEND-P1, REFUTING BOTH BRAINSTORM panic hypotheses) — falls back to a flat, health-ignoring, host-count-uniform draw across every host in every tier, reusing the EXISTING `lb_healthy_panic` stat. The construct decomposes into three unit-testable pure functions (`tierHealth` — a panic-DISABLED per-tier health VIEW, AMEND-P1-COROLLARY, ensuring no individual tier can internally flatten itself, a documented improvement over `locality.go`'s own accepted-but-unfixed child-local-panic coverage boundary; `tierCapacity`; `cascadeLoads`) plus `priorityGroup`/`priorityLB`, both constructors (`newPriorityLB`/`newPriorityLBWithRNG`), the phase-53-LOCAL `priorityLeafFactory` type (kept deliberately separate from `subset.go`'s shared `leafFactory`, D-P-FACTORY), and the real `Pick` — which needs **ZERO new `Pick` parameters** (`loadBalancer.Pick(hashKey, hasHash, match, hasMatch)` stays byte-for-byte unchanged; the flat fallback child is built with health=NIL, a deliberate departure from `localityWeightedLB`'s own shared-health flat child, since priorityLB's differently-shaped bypass condition cannot guarantee agreement with a leaf's own internal panic check). `Endpoint` grows a `Priority uint32` field (the THIRD per-endpoint dimension after phase-38's `Metadata` and phase-52's `Locality`/`LocalityWeight`), captured by an extended `extractEndpoints` (`manager.go:834`, the SOLE production keyed-literal edit — the `Endpoint{}` construction-site sweep re-confirmed 1 edit site out of 101 `internal/cluster` hits + 44 `internal/filter` hits, all others bare-zero or field-disjoint keyed literals). `manager.go`'s `buildCluster` gains a THIRD wrap-after-switch site (alongside the phase-38.1 `lb_subset_config` and phase-52 `locality_weighted_lb_config` wraps): a `distinctPriorities` helper gates the wrap so it fires ONLY for a multi-tier-priority cluster, guarded by TWO new envoy-go-strict rejects (multi-tier `priority` combined with `locality_weighted_lb_config` rejected; multi-tier `priority` combined with `lb_subset_config` rejected — BOTH confirmed live departures from a reference that MEANINGFULLY COMPOSES both, AMEND-P2), plus a SECOND health-registry-widening side effect (D-P-HEALTHALLOC — the D-LW-HEALTHALLOC precedent reapplied to the SAME two pre-existing stat NAMES `membership_healthy`/`lb_healthy_panic`, a widened CONDITION not a stat-surface count change). **TWO genuine issues were found and fixed DURING implementation, neither papered over:** (1) a **Task 1 documentation-accuracy fix** — the construction-site-sweep anchors carried over from the PLAN template cited wrong file names/counts, corrected against the real worktree before proceeding; (2) a **Task 9/10 flake investigation** — the first `-count=20` batch of the `0096` fixture (against the Task 9 baseline, BEFORE any Task 10 changes) was NOT clean, an intermittent ~1-in-20-to-1-in-60 reference-side arm(a) failure (`tally.t1 != 0`, small counts 3–29/300). Live diagnostic instrumentation conclusively RULED OUT health-check flapping (`health_check.failure` stayed 0, `membership_healthy` stayed 10 in failing runs), and the leaked requests clustered at the very START of the 300-request load phase, immediately after `warmupUntilStable`'s K-consecutive-success streak — i.e. K=10 was occasionally satisfied BY CHANCE before the underlying selection state had genuinely stabilized (THREE falsified hypotheses first: widening the health-check timeout alone made it WORSE, widening interval+timeout proportionally didn't help, pinning a long `dns_refresh_rate` to rule out STRICT_DNS re-resolution didn't help). **Root-cause fix:** raised `warmupStable` from **10 → 60** in the `0096` driver (widening the SAME mechanism Task 9 built — NOT a magic-number retry or a sleep), confirmed clean over 4 further `-count=20` batches (80/80 PASS) plus both deliberate breaks re-verified against the fixed state. The `0096-lb-priority` cross-side differential (a 2-tier × 5-host topology + a driver-owned toggle responder for tier 0) proves BOTH arms live: (a) a hard **100%/0%** all-healthy static split and (b) a hard **0%/100%** full-failover split (a deliberately narrower proof shape than locality-weighted's banded-ratio pair, since failover is a hard boundary — the user's Q4 choice, avoiding the band-margin re-tuning risk phases 35/52 both hit), each 20/20 flake-free and `-race` clean via TWO controller-reperformed deliberate breaks (drop `Priority:` from the `extractEndpoints` literal → the warmup gate times out; skip `SetHealthy(false)` in arm(b) → `membership_healthy` never converges to 5), each `-count=1` (`reference_differential_break_protocol_count1` / `reference_differential_run_selector`) failing exactly as predicted then restored. **The final whole-branch review verdict: Ready to merge — Yes**, with 2 trivial cosmetic doc findings, one of which (the ADR-0270 §Context mislabeling the full-failover arm as a hard 100%/0% split instead of the correct **0%/100%** — the arm's own §heading, `BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT.md`, `expectations.yaml`, and the driver code all agree on 0%/100%) was fixed in a final small commit (`df848be5`).
